Nobody sees another's values: a sponsor counts on others by their households' lots alone. **Taking states moved to midnight:** everyone takes their values and norms the first midnight they are here (the founders on the first night, a child the night after their birth), not on the first of the month: the curfew test lives only three days, and found that until 1 April nobody held the norm, so a curfew weighed nothing. **The ten-year smoke passed all 10 worlds:** values held -0.19 to +0.29 on average and 19 to 68 % strongly (|v| ≥ 0.5) in villages of 10 to 50; what people hold dear weighed (beyond the stance margin) in 24 to 54 % of the stances taken; villages proposed 2 to 6 laws in ten years, as before (2 to 13 under norms), and three amended their custom once each. In the government end-to-end world, a village amended its custom to two-thirds, 23 to 0. **Boundary:** saves schema 39 (38 still loads: everyone draws their values the next midnight), wire 1.38 (each person's values in the inspector, the values part of a recorded stance), content API 43. **Gates:** Gate B and the notables' gate passed. **Not built:** values that move with experience, status and obligations beyond kin (06-04 §1.1's other values), values seen or guessed by others, and values weighing in anything but laws.
- **Ideologies, as built (2026-10-08, M4c slice AG step four, ADR-0016 §4):** no new ADR. **An ideology is content** (`kind = "ideology"`, content API 44), after research 06-04 §6.1's record: the problem it explains (an issue the polity already names), its commitments (how it tilts each value its holders hold, -1 to 1), its program (the policy templates it proposes), its legitimacy story in words, and how it travels. The core pack names three: common provision (explains food short; safety 0.5, giving back 0.3; a common store and a storekeeper), order kept by all (explains takings; safety 0.4, giving back 0.4; a law against taking and a watch) and each household its own (explains being overruled; a household's say 0.6; no program). **Holding one:** the first midnight someone is here, one with no parents recorded (a founder, a newcomer) brings each ideology by a keyed chance of 0.1, and a child takes up what a parent holds by a chance of 0.5 (06-04 §1.7); the record keeps when and from whom. At the hearth a holder speaks of one by a keyed chance of 0.0005 a session, and a listener who does not hold it takes it up by 0.3 × their trust in the teller × how well its commitments fit what they hold dear (the sum of value × commitment, clipped to 0 to 1), so one that runs against what they hold is never taken up (06-04 §1.4: exposure is not acceptance). Nobody gives one up yet. **What it does:** a holder weighs every law with the ideology's commitments as if they were values they hold, in their anchor, stance and weighing of proposing it; and when the problem it explains is before the village (06-04 §1.2: an explanation shapes the answer to a problem that is felt, not the problem itself) the templates its program names join their moves with a point (`w_program`) for proposing them. A law proposed by one who holds an ideology naming it keeps that creed (saved apart from news claims, not "spread as a claim" as ADR-0016 sketched: a creed is a holding, not an account of an event), and the Government panel says it was proposed "as one who holds to" it. **Two findings on the way.** First, programs weighed whether or not their problem was present: holders proposed stores in villages never short of food, and eleven tests broke; programs now wait on the problem. Second, with adoption by trust alone, every ideology reached 31 to 52 of about 50 people in ten years, everyone holding everything; the fit gate stopped that, and at an adoption chance of 0.1 only 3 to 12 held each, so the chance was set at 0.3 (a design prior, chosen by this calibration). **The ten-year smoke passed all 10 worlds:** each ideology was held by 0 to 35 of 17 to 56 people (most often a sixth to a half; each household its own died out in one valley); 0.019 to 0.071 spoken of and up to 0.002 taken up a person a week; 15 of the 29 laws proposed were proposed by someone holding an ideology that names them (14 passed: common stores under common provision, one law against taking under order kept by all), and villages proposed 2 to 6 laws in ten years, as before. That a holder's creed marks a law does not show the creed caused it: most were stores proposed when food was short, which the household's own lot already favoured. **Boundary:** saves schema 40 (39 still loads: everyone takes their start the next midnight), section `creeds` (eight-byte tags), wire 1.39 (each person's ideologies in the inspector, a law's creed in its issue), content API 44. **Gates:** Gate B and the notables' gate passed. **Not built:** giving one up or turning against it, ideologies made or recombined by people (06-04 §1.7), organizations that teach one (ADR-0016 §4), the god tool that introduces one (slice AJ), and programs that name amendments of the custom.
- **Factions, as built (2026-10-08, M4c slice AH step one, ADR-0017 §2):** no new ADR (ADR-0017 decided the organization, its treasury on the ledger and memberships on persons). **A faction** organizes those who hold grievances against one party, the gathering or an office, and records its founder, organizer, store and history. **Founding** (04-10 §1.1, §1.5; never because enough people are angry, 04-06 §1.8): on their review day, an adult who belongs to none, knows of no faction against that party and feels their keenest grievance against the gathering or an office at `found_floor` 0.3 or more weighs founding one at `w_grievance` 1 × how keenly they feel it + `w_shared` 1 × how far it is shared (the others they regard at `trust_floor` 0.25 or more whom they have heard hold one against the same party, each counted once however often told, of `shared_full` 3) − `found_cost` 0.5, against a threshold of their own drawn evenly from 0.5 to 2 by a key (04-10 §1.3); with nobody to share it, never. **Joining and staying:** each adult reviews where they belong on a day of their own each month (`review_days` 30; 09-12 §2.2). Belonging is worth `w_grievance` × their grievance against its party + `w_organizer` 0.5 × their regard for its organizer + `w_belong` 1 × the share of those they know who belong (the count beside it, 04-10 §5.2) − `w_dues` 5 × `dues_share` 0.05; one who belongs nowhere joins the faction they know of (whose organizer or a member they know) worth most, if it beats their threshold, and a member leaves only when it falls `leave_margin` 0.5 below it (04-10 §5.4). Membership is kept apart from belief: a member's grievance may fade while they stay, and someone who holds one may never join (04-10 §5.1). Every number is a design prior; no report gives a founding size or a joining rate. **The store** is a holder on the ledger, kept at its organizer's home: a member's household gives `dues_share` of each threshing (channel `dues`, appended as code 17) while the store holds less than `reserve_days` 20 of its members' households' food, unless it cannot spare it (the levy's test), and a member's household short of food may ask it, as it asks the common store, for up to `aid_days` 5 of its food (04-10 §1.2: mutual assistance; channel `gift`). Its longest-standing member takes up its organizing when the organizer dies, leaves or quits it; one with no members ends, and what its store holds goes to its last organizer's household. **Three findings on the way.** First, dues without a bound: stores reached 2,026 and 3,766 kg in ten years and gave nothing, because members' households never fell below five days of food after they joined (an instrumented coast village found no member's household asking for food in eighteen months); dues now stop at the reserve. Second, a founder counted full regard for themselves as organizer, which kept founders nobody joined in their own factions (a village with factions of 13, 1, 1 and 1); without it, a founder nobody joined quit and founded again the next month (117 foundings in one village), so one whose faction ended does not found another within `retry_days` 365 (04-10 §3: a failed attempt lowers what one expects of the next). Third, a smoke run left two coast villages at 16 and 14 people where earlier runs had 41 to 50; the same two seeds with fixed identities, factions on and off, ended at 44 against 42 and 42 against 42, so it was the draw of the worlds' lives (a smoke world's identity is new each run), not factions. **The ten-year smoke passed all 10 worlds:** factions formed in the three hungry coast villages and nowhere else (the valleys hold no grievances): one of 26 members in a village of 48; two with members at the end (12 and 1) of 8 founded in another, a lone founder trying again each year; one of 4 in a village down to 6, with 29 joinings and 25 leavings as people died and left. Dues came to 251 to 742 kg a world and the stores gave out 0 to 108 kg. **Boundary:** saves schema 41 (section `factions`; 40 still loads, with none), wire 1.40 (a person's faction and why in the inspector, kernel words; each polity's factions in the Government panel), content API 45 (`[faction]`). **Gates:** Gate B and the notables' gate passed. **Not built:** a faction's program and demands, petitions, assemblies, refusals of a levy, damage and violence (steps two and three), splits, factions over an ideology alone, belonging to two, and spending the store on anything but aid.
- **Petitions, as built (2026-10-08, M4c slice AH step two, ADR-0017 §3):** no new ADR (ADR-0017 §3 decided episodes: coming is a scored choice and the answer is the gathering's). **Calling** (research 09-04 §5.5: petitioning is one response beside complying, evading and leaving; 09-05 §1.2: petitioning is a right apart from proposing): at their monthly review a faction's organizer weighs calling a petition at the hearth once it has `petition_members` 3 members, none of its petitions waits and its last was called `petition_days` 180 or more days ago, at `w_grievance` 1 × its members' mean grievance against its party + the share of those the organizer knows who belong − `petition_cost` 0.5, against their own threshold. **Its demand answers the party blamed** (04-10 §1.1): against the gathering, the common store at another of its template's shares, or at none, in place of the one in force, whichever its members' households forecast most for, and only if they forecast it to bring them something; against an office, another holder in place of the one in it, whom the organizer would name as any proposer names a keeper (themselves if their roof would do, or whoever under one they regard most; for the watch, any adult), never the one blamed. A law may now name the law it replaces (`ends`), and passing it supersedes that law, so no new policy kind was needed; a store at no share levies nothing and still gives what it holds, and since a store in force keeps ordinary proposers from proposing another, only a petition changes it. **Word** goes from the organizer to its members the day it is called, then by word of mouth as a gathering's call does (a third claim kind, `petition`, let go once it has sat); it sits the next evening (`notice_days`). **Coming** is a scored activity (`core:activity/petition`, behaviour `petition`) for an adult who heard of it, on the evening it sits, with 04-10 §5.3's terms from their own records: `w_grievance` × their grievance against its party, `w_member` 1 if they belong or else `w_organizer` 0.5 × their regard for its organizer (identification and organization), and `w_expect` 1 × f(S), S the share of those they know, by how well, who belong; f(S) = S, or 4S(1 − S) for the keyed `free_ride_share` 0.2 of people for whom more others coming adds less once half do (04-10 §1.4: strategic substitutability; the shape is a design prior). It competes with everything else they might do that evening, and a curfew in force weighs against it (only the watch and those at a gathering are exempt). **The answer:** at the first midnight after it sat with no gathering waiting, the organizer proposes the demand as called (issue `petition`), those who came learn of the proposal, and the gathering decides it as any law. For a new officeholder, each who came weighs their regard for the one named less their regard for the one in office, which a grievance against the office lowers (the holder defends their place at full regard). A petition turned down, split or too thinly attended to decide is a wrong (`refused`, felt as `refused_days` 5 of a household's food) that each who came holds against the gathering (04-10 §3: failed petitioning is a precursor); one nobody came to ends there, and one whose law in force changed or whose organizer is gone is never put. A petition records who came (unique participants, 04-10 §4.2); it sits one evening, so participant-days equal them and the peak and duration are not kept. **Two findings on the way.** First, the first build let only factions against the gathering petition, and its ten-year smoke called none: a probe of the three coast villages with factions (11 to 23 members) found every faction against the storekeeper (an empty store is held against its keeper), so the demand now follows the party blamed. Second, those who came then abstained at the gathering (one coast village called 15 petitions in ten years, 13 of them split, one person for and one against), because nothing in their stance held their grievance; a grievance against an office now lowers regard for its holder in that decision. **Also fixed:** a keeper whose own household found the store empty held it against their own keeping; they now hold it against the gathering. **The ten-year smoke passed all 10 worlds.** Factions formed only in the three hungry coast villages, as before. In two of them organizers called petitions, all for another keeper: in one, 22 came and the gathering granted it; in the other, 16 and 17 came to two, one granted and one without a quorum, so 16 held its refusal against the gathering. The third (a faction of 12) called none. A run of the same build before the smoke report counted replacements had petitions in all three (one each with 24 and 23 who came, granted; two with 14 and 27, one granted, one without a quorum). No petition was for a store's share, and none was turned down by a vote. One coast village ended at 26 people where four earlier runs of its seed today ended at 46 to 53: not probed; the world's identity is drawn anew each run and step one's fixed-identity comparison found such swings were the draw. **Boundary:** saves schema 42 (laws' `ends`; petitions in `factions`; 41 still loads, with none), wire 1.41 (each polity's petitions in the Government panel, in kernel words), content API 46 (the `petition` activity and seven `[faction]` keys). **Gates:** Gate B and the notables' gate passed. **Not built:** assemblies with other demands (another template, an amendment of the custom), what petitioners expect it to achieve and the sanction they expect (04-10 §5.3's E and pD), a grievance weighing at the gathering on a store's share (households weigh only what they forecast), refusals of a levy, damage and violence (step three).
- **Refusals of a levy, as built (2026-10-08, M4c slice AH step three, ADR-0017 §3):** no new ADR. ADR-0017 §3 names three kinds of episode (petition, assembly, refusal of a levy); this is the second built, after the petition. **Calling** (research 09-04 §5.5: refusing beside petitioning and leaving; 04-10 §1.9: a withdrawal works through what it withholds): at their monthly review, when no petition is called (one waits, the last was within `petition_days`, nothing answers, or it is not worth it), an organizer whose members blame the gathering or the store's keeper weighs calling on them to keep back the levy of the store in force together, openly, for `refusal_days` 365. It needs a store that levies something, `petition_members` members, and none of the faction's petitions waiting nor refusals standing. Its worth is what a petition's would be (`w_grievance` × the members' mean grievance + the share of those the organizer knows who belong), less `refusal_cost` 0.5 (as calling a petition costs) and less what the norms the organizer holds weigh for abiding by what the gathering decided. Where a petition can be called it is always worth more, and in a village that holds the gathering binds (about 1.4 points on average) a refusal is rare. **Word** of it goes from the organizer to its members, then by word of mouth (a fourth claim kind, `refusal`, let go when it ends). **Joining** is each thresher's choice at their own threshing, for one who heard of it: what joining is worth to them, with the same terms as coming to a petition (grievance against its party, belonging or regard for the organizer, the share of those they know who belong, free-riding for some), is taken from the points for paying, so it weighs toward keeping back. A levy kept back by one who heard of a refusal is counted on the law as kept back in a refusal (`refused`, `refused_kg`), apart from one kept back unannounced (`evaded`; `withheld_kg` counts both); the refusal records who kept back and how much. Their household tells companions it did not pay, which moves others' belief of how many abide, as any keeping back does (M4c slice AG). **Answer:** none beyond what the gathering already does. Nobody collects a levy (households pay at their own threshing), so a refusal meets no one; the store has less to give, and members may petition again when they can. A test village with no one holding the norm called a refusal of a fifth's levy; at the harvest 29 kept back 355 kg under it while 6 levies were paid. **The first prior was wrong:** with `refusal_cost` 1 a refusal was worth less than its organizer's threshold even with no norm held (0.65 against 0.78 in the test), so it could hardly ever be called. Since what sets refusing apart from petitioning is the norm it breaks, the cost is now a petition's. **The ten-year smoke passed all 10 worlds** on a second run. The first run failed one coast world's 30-day check: a household was without water at a day's end, the rare gap recorded twice before (1 in about 35 runs). Nothing this step changed runs before a village has a store and a faction. No refusal was called in either run: every organizer held the norm or could petition. Petitions were called in three coast villages, five in all, every one to replace a keeper and every one granted (9 to 28 came); one village replaced its keeper three times in ten years as the store ran empty again. **A probe:** one coast village ended at 49, 26, 32 and 16 people in the four runs since keeper petitions began, against 46 to 53 before, so the same seed was lived ten years from four fixed identities with petitions on and off. On and off ended at 42 and 39, 45 and 45, 33 and 30, and 17 and 17 people: petitions did not cost the village people, and the spread is the identity's draw. **Boundary:** saves schema 43 (laws' `refused`, `refused_kg`; refusals in `factions`; 42 still loads), wire 1.42 (a law's `refused`; each polity's refusals in the Government panel, in kernel words), content API 47 (`refusal_cost`, `refusal_days`). **Gates:** Gate B and the notables' gate passed. **Not built:** an answer to a refusal (a sanction proposed, the store's keeper collecting), refusing other obligations, and damage and violence, moved to slice AI (below).
- **Damage and violence wait for an office of force (2026-10-08, plan decision):** ADR-0017 §3 has damage to property and violence against people as separate choices within an episode, and the plan put them in AH's step three. They move to slice AI, which brings the first office of force. Violence is an interaction process that answers encounters with those who disperse, collect or repress (04-10 §1.8), and none of these villages has anyone who does: a levy is paid at each household's own threshing, the watch walks rounds against takings, and nobody commands force. Built now, a per-person choice to damage or harm within a petition or a refusal would either never fire or fire without cause, which would be plot. 06-10 §4 also asks that harm the engine can produce be answerable (who did what to whom, under which decision, and what followed); that wants the incident records of an office's orders, which AI brings. Not an ADR: ADR-0017 is unchanged, only the slice that builds it.
- **Founding, as built (2026-10-08, M4c slice AI step two, ADR-0017 §5):** no new ADR (ADR-0017 §5 decided founding as bargaining from what was inherited). **The window:** for `founding_days` 90 after the custom is taken from the gathering (a design prior: research 09-11 §1.6's founding processes run 3 to 36 months and none is forced to finish), while some law the old custom made is still unweighed, the issue `founding` is before the village. **Representation** (09-11 §1.6: through institutions that exist): every member of the body that took the deciding deliberates at each weekly review in the window, not only its notables. **Proposals** (09-11 §1.6: packages built from the inherited arrangements): a new policy kind `repeal` (content `core:policy/repeal`, answering `founding`) opens to each of them an end to each law the old custom made: a store's levy at none, as a petition would end it (the store gives what it holds), or any other law repealed. **Weighing:** each household weighs an end as what the law, kept, would bring it, turned about, and what the law does to what its people hold dear (values, and a creed that proposes it), turned about too; the new body decides it as any law (its own procedure, the ratification). A repeal that passes is *carried* (a new law status), the law it ends superseded; an office ended leaves a vacancy the ordinary review may fill. **Each law is put once:** what the body leaves, or turns down, stands (provisional authority, unless actors dismantle it). **A fix in passing:** a petition to end a store's levy had weighed what people hold dear as if it were a store; any end to a law now weighs it turned about. **Test:** after the revolt of step one held, the old custom's heavy levy and a curfew were both put within two weeks by members of the new body, and both passed (22 for and 1 against; 24 for and none against): the levy at none, the curfew repealed and carried; it saves and loads exactly. **The ten-year smoke passed all 10 worlds** on the first run; no revolt was called, so no founding (three petitions in two villages, all for another keeper, all granted). **Boundary:** saves schema 45 (laws' status `carried` and issue `founding`; 44 still loads), wire 1.44 (status 5, carried; the web says "superseded by a later law" for any law a later one replaced, not only an amendment), content API 49 (`repeal`, `founding`, `[polity] founding_days`, `core:policy/repeal`). **Gates:** Gate B and the notables' gate passed. **Not built:** bargaining among factions over packages of provisions (09-11 §1.6's U_f), the old gathering as a second representative body, reserved privileges for the losers, appointments as a founding move of their own (an office ended is filled by the ordinary review), and a founding after a coup (step three).
- **A watch of several and coups, as built (2026-10-08, M4c slice AI step three, ADR-0017 §4):** no new ADR (ADR-0017 §4 decided that a coup is open only to those holding an office that commands force, the watch when it is several, with no roll). **A watch of several:** while takings continue (food taken from a household since the newest watcher was named) the watch template stays open with a watch in force, so a further watcher may be proposed, each named by a law of their own, and only by one it would serve (as an amendment); what one more adds is what the others leave unguarded, `g(1 − g)^n` of the guard `g` one watcher gives beside `n` others (independent rounds; a forecast, not a measured rate), so each further watcher is worth less. Every watcher walks rounds and sees; a further one's rounds start past those before. **A coup** (research 09-11 §1.4: willingness and coordination apart): at their monthly review, a watcher of several who holds a grievance against the gathering weighs calling on the others to take the deciding for the watch, worth their grievance and how well the other watchers regard them (officers act on what they believe the others will do), less `coup_cost` 0.75 and the norms they hold. Not where the watch decides already, nor while a coup or a revolt stands there. **Sides:** each midnight the watchers, and only they, stand with it, with the gathering or with neither, as anyone stands with a revolt (their grievance, their regard for the one who called it, and the share of the other watchers with it the day before), less the norms they hold. **Holding:** once more watchers stand with it than with the gathering for `hold_days` 7 (09-11 §2.2's coding), the body becomes those who keep the watch (a new membership, `watch`), by the custom's own quorum and rule; the custom is taken, its version naming the one who called it, and the founding of step two follows. It comes to nothing when its caller no longer keeps the watch, the watch is no longer several, or after `revolt_days`. The rest of the village takes no side: without force (step four) nothing they do bears on it. **Test:** three watchers who all held a grievance and regarded one another well: one called a coup at their review, all three stood with it, and it held a week later, the watch deciding thereafter; it saves and loads exactly. A watch of two each walked their own rounds. **The first build was wrong:** with the template simply left open, the first ten-year smoke had one coast village name 18 watchers (17 at once, 80,094 rounds in ten years) and another 28: a move worth almost nothing is still proposed now and then (the choice among moves is soft, against a proposal's cost), and it passes on regard for the one it names. Before this step a law in force closed its template, which hid that. Requiring that takings continued since the newest watcher was named, and that the sponsor gains by it, brought the rerun to one watch in one village and three named over ten years in another. **The ten-year smoke passed all 10 worlds** on both runs; no coup was called (no village had a watch of several when a watcher reviewed with a grievance), and three petitions were, all granted. **Two tests changed:** a crime test that assumed one watcher now accepts a further watcher the village named while takings went on (it did, in that test); and the ideology test that asked companions to take up an ideology within a month failed about 1 run in 20 because, for some worlds' identities, it fitted nobody who heard it (0 of 337 tellings taken up), so the test now has everyone hold dear what the most-held ideology commits them to. **Boundary:** saves schema 46 (coups, kept as revolt records with no faction; 45 still loads), wire 1.45 (each polity's coups in the Government panel, in kernel words), content API 50 (`coup_cost`; the membership `watch`). A decision's tally now names the body's members ("3 of 4 watchers came"). **Gates:** Gate B and the notables' gate passed. **Not built:** leaving the watch in a coup (09-11 §1.4's fragmenting), which waits for step four, where staying has a cost; resistance by anyone but the watchers; a coup by the storekeeper; and counter-coups beyond what any later coup or revolt can do.
- **Force, as built (2026-10-08, M4c slice AI step four, ADR-0017 §3–§4):** no new ADR (ADR-0017 §3 has violence as a person's choice within an episode and §4 the office of force; the plan decision above moved damage and violence to this slice). **Going to collect** (research 09-06 §1.3: carrying out an order is a stage of its own): at their monthly review, one who keeps the watch weighs going to take what a gathering's finding owed and the household refused (an obligation a case imposed, still owed, on a household of their settlement other than their own, and not gone to within the faction review's span). It is worth `w_collect` 0.5, the norms they hold (what the gathering decides binds), and their regard for the household owed (none for a fine, which goes to the common store) less their regard for the household owing, against their own threshold. **How they are met** (04-10 §1.8: violence answers encounters, and is each person's choice): each adult of the household lets it be taken, worth the norms they hold and `w_regard` × their regard for the watcher, or resists, worth how strongly their household refused it and their grievances against the watch and the gathering. One whose will to resist passes their own reluctance to strike, keyed per person in `strike_threshold` [1, 4] (a design prior), strikes; the others who resist stand in the way. **Force:** where anyone resisted, the watcher takes it by force if it is still worth it less `w_harm` 1 for each who did, or turns back. What is taken is the household's food up to what is still owed, whatever it keeps back, and goes where the finding said, on the obligation's channel (an execution takes what is held, 09-06 §5.4). **Blows** (06-10 §4.C: every injury and death resolves to a record and a cause): only where it was taken by force, each who struck strikes the watcher and the watcher strikes back. A blow keeps the one struck from work for 3 to 21 days (`hurt_days`, drawn; they still sleep, eat, fetch water, keep company, rest, ask, and come to gatherings and petitions, with the reason "a blow keeps them from work"), and kills with `kill_share` 0.02, a design prior (no report gives a rate for a blow among villagers): a death by violence, "died of a blow". **What it leaves** (04-10 §1.7): each adult of the household holds a grievance against the watch for what was taken by force and for each blow the watcher struck; whoever is struck thinks the worse of the one who struck them (a tie act, `struck`, a bad act in provision at weight 3). The chronicle tells the encounter in plain verbs, nothing justified (06-10 §4.C–D: who did what to whom, under which decision, and what followed): "Dara, who keeps the watch, came to the household of Brisa to take what a finding of the gathering owed; Brisa and Vane met Dara with blows; Dara took 4 days' food for the household of Tam by force; Brisa struck Dara, who was kept from work 15 days; …". The Order panel lists encounters newest first. **Tests:** a watcher went to a household that had refused a finding, was met with blows, took what was owed by force, and every blow was recorded with its days, the household's grievances against the watch and the tie written, saving and loading exactly; with `kill_share` at 1 a blow killed, a death by violence with its record. **The ten-year smoke passed all 10 worlds**; no village named a watch or refused a finding this run, so no watcher went to collect and force is shown by the tests alone. **Boundary:** saves schema 47 (encounters on the order; 46 still loads), wire 1.46 (the Order panel's encounters, in kernel words), content API 51 (`w_collect`, `w_harm`, `strike_threshold`, `hurt_days`, `kill_share`; the tie act `struck`). **Gates:** Gate B and the notables' gate passed. **Not built:** keeping a seized store (a coup or a revolt changes the body by its holders' choices, and the store's keeper only by law); damage to property; fear or deterrence from a blow seen or heard of (ties' `fear` is still not written); the inspector showing how long a hurt person is kept from work; and whether those of the household are at home when the watcher comes (every adult of it meets them).
- **A whisper and an ideology heard of, as built (2026-10-08, M4c slice AJ step one, ADR-0016 §5):** no new ADR (ADR-0016 §5 decided the god tools: each submits only what a person could perceive, one record per recipient and proposition, every use logged). **The record:** each use of a god tool is an intervention record (`civ_agents::influence`): its number, when first and last used, how often, its kind, whom it reached and what it submitted; a repeat to the same person with the same thing refreshes that record and never makes another (research 15-05 §4.5: a hundred identical whispers equal one). The chronicle logs each first use as one recorded influence ("One recorded influence: the observer whispered to Mara that Bo holds a grievance against the gathering over what was taken from their household."), and nothing in the world reads the records. **The whisper:** a true claim the adult's settlement's word holds and they have not heard (a gathering or a petition called and still to sit, a refusal or a revolt declared and still standing, or another's grievance someone has told), placed in their hearing from no one. What they do with it is what anyone who heard it would do: come to a gathering or a petition, join a refusal, take a side in a revolt, or, for a grievance against a party they also blame, count one more who shares it when they weigh founding a faction (04-10 §1.1). Its salience is the claim's own: a call lapses once its day is past and a grievance after `news_days` 60 untold, two of the monthly reviews that read it (15-05 §5's one to three decision cycles). It is not passed on as news unless it is a call; a grievance is told only by those who hold it. **An ideology heard of** (as `IntroduceTechnique { aware_only }` makes a technique known): the adult weighs it as one heard at the hearth is, by how well its commitments fit what they hold dear (06-04 §1.4: exposure is not acceptance), with no teller to doubt, so the chance is the content's `adopt` × the fit, none unless it fits at all (a design prior: a stranger's word would carry nothing, a trusted teller's the most). The draw is keyed to the person and the ideology alone, so telling them again draws nothing new; they weigh it again at each monthly review while it is fresh (within `news_days` of the last telling), by the same draw, so only a change in what they hold dear can change the answer (15-05 §4.5: new context can matter). One who takes it up holds it as anyone does and may speak of it at the hearth. **What came of it** (15-05 §6): the inspector's new section, the observer's hand, lists each influence on a person with what followed, read from the records people keep and drawing nothing: for a whisper, when it was heard, how many others they told, and whether they came, joined, kept back their levy or stood with a revolt, or founded or joined a faction against the party whose grievance they heard; for an ideology, how often they weighed it and when they took it up, or how well it fits them now and the chance that carries, and how many took it up from them. The news a person has not heard, and the ideologies, are offered there to whisper and to tell; a child cannot be reached, and a claim they heard already, or an ideology they hold, is refused. **Tests:** a whisper of another's grievance is heard from no one, logged once, and a hundred repeats leave one record of 101 uses and one chronicle entry; one who has heard it, and a child, are refused; it wrote no grievance, faction, law or holding; an ideology told of to one it does not fit is not taken up however often told (one record, weighed once), one it fits takes it up from no one, and the first takes it up at a review a month after what they hold dear changed; all of it saves and loads exactly; a schema-47 save loads with no records; the end-to-end test tells an adult of common provision from the inspector and a repeat adds nothing. **The ten-year smoke passed all 10 worlds** (it uses no god tool, and one the observer never reached weighs nothing more at their review). **Boundary:** saves schema 48 (the `influenc` section; 47 still loads), wire 1.47 (the commands `Whisper` and `TellOfIdeology`; the welcome's ideologies; a person's news and influences; a heard claim's and a holding's influence), no content API change. **Gates:** Gate B and the notables' gate passed. **Not built:** the whisper of a deliberator's move (the M4 observer brief's other reading of 15-05; ADR-0016 §5 chose a true claim), the truth of a taking whispered as a belief (ADR-0015's beliefs keep their own sources), an ideology seen by the witnesses of a public sign (15-05 §5's 25–100), and "considered" for a whisper, which would need decisions to record what they read.
- **The agitator, blessings and curses, as built (2026-10-08, M4c slice AJ step two, ADR-0016 §5):** no new ADR. **The agitator** (ADR-0016 §5: a newcomer holding an ideology, sent by the path that sends a family, an ordinary person bound by every law, starting with a stranger's ties; settled in the M4a design that an agitator is not a person rewritten): the map tool sends one adult, 20 to 40 years old, with a household of their own and a newcomer's provisions, where a family would go (joining a settlement within reach, else making camp). They hold the ideology chosen from the day they come, as one who brought it (and, like any newcomer, whatever else the content's founders' shares give them that midnight), know nobody, and are moved by no special disposition: whether anyone takes it up from them is the hearth's ordinary telling and trust, which a stranger has none of. The chronicle tells it as one recorded influence ("…the observer sent Sela, who holds to order kept by all, to Hazelford.") in place of a family's arrival, and the inspector says how many took it up from them and how many they know there now. A family's making is unchanged; a household of one has no couple, partner or union. **A blessing or a curse** (the brief's proposal, research 15-05 being silent on luck: for a stated period, the target's own keyed draws for named material purposes): for 30, 90, 365 or 1,825 days, the person's own chance of illness or accident (the baseline hazard; hunger's part is the body's, never luck's) is scaled by `1 − share` for a blessing and by `1 / (1 − share)` for a curse, and their chance of finding a technique out the other way about, at most 1; the share is a little (0.1), a quarter or a half (0.05 to 0.5 accepted, so a chance is at most halved or doubled). The draw itself is unchanged and nothing else is drawn, so what it turned is exact: the same draw against the chance without it. Each death it spared or brought, and each find it brought or cost, is counted on its record and told in the chronicle ("One recorded influence: Arrel was spared a death by illness or accident: the observer's blessing turned the draw."). It never touches a decision's draw, a vote, a tie, detection or punishment, nor food, harvests or buildings: those draws are not the person's own (a building's quality is drawn for the building, and spoilage and making are not drawn at all). A blessing of one already blessed refreshes it (its period at least the new one from today) and never stacks; a curse ends a blessing that day, and the other way about. A child may be blessed; the whisper and the ideology reach adults only. **Tests:** an agitator sent to the hearth joins the village alone, an adult with no partner or union, holding the ideology from no one and knowing nobody, logged once and not as a family; sent into the water or with no such ideology, refused; it saves and loads exactly. With the constant adult hazard raised by hand (80 a year), half a village's adults blessed and half cursed at half the way for 30 days: within 10 days blessings had spared deaths and curses brought them, every turned draw had its chronicle entry, a share out of range or a period of none was refused, a second blessing refreshed the first, and a blessing given to one cursed ended the curse that day; a schema-48 save loads with no one blessed; the end-to-end test blesses an adult from the inspector and sends an agitator with the map tool. **The ten-year smoke passed all 10 worlds** (it uses no god tool). **Boundary:** saves schema 49 (an influence's period, share and what it turned; 48 still loads), wire 1.48 (the commands `SendAgitator` and `Bless`), no content API change. **Gates:** Gate B and the notables' gate passed. **Not built:** a blessing of a household's or a firm's fortunes (harvests, spoilage, a building's quality: draws that are not a person's own, or not drawn), and an agitator with an organizer's disposition (the M4 factions brief's proposal; ADR-0016 §5 makes them ordinary).
- **The regimes row and the M4 demo, as built (2026-10-09, M4c slice AJ step three):** no new ADR. **The regimes row** (plan §4.7: "two seeds differ"; the M4 observer brief: "differ" only where the label's reasons name a structural difference, the classifier frozen before the first run, no reference rate of divergence): each world's polity is graded by its label's principal name without the person it names ("Council community", "Oligarchy — big-man leadership"), which says who may decide and who leads, with the first of its reasons; green when two worlds end under different names, amber when they do not ("reported and not forced"), grey with fewer than two worlds labelled. The row also shows each tenth year's names where they changed. The classifier (ADR-0013 §6, M4a slice Z) was not touched for it, and it does not read the property regime, which the worlds take in turn. **The M4 demo** (`web/e2e/m4-demo.spec.ts`, run with `TCE_DEMO=1`): the dashboard keeps each world's save at every tenth year's end (`--keep-saves`), each world in a folder of its own, so the folder it kept them in is a saves folder the observer opens; the demo loads each world's save of year 40, shows the Government panel (the custom, what it would be called and why, its laws), opens the oldest decided law of the world whose gathering decided the most to its whole history, and logs them side by side with the number of regimes. A world whose regime changed after year 40, by the dashboard's report, is shown again at year 50 with the newest law its body decided opened: a coda past the demo's forty years, labelled so. Without kept saves, the demo runs the dashboard for forty years first. **A display fault, found by the demo and fixed:** the Government panel told every decision's tally with the members of the body now ("34 of 40 elders came" for an amendment the adults decided before it made the elders the body); a decision is now told under the version of the custom in force before it (`Polity::body_at`), so that one reads "34 of 40 adults came". **Tests:** the row is amber for one regime, green for two with the decade it changed, grey for one world; a decision is told under the body that decided it, the amendment that changed the body under the one before. **The run and what it showed:** the next entry.
- **The M4 demo and the dashboard after M4c (2026-10-09):** `civ-host dashboard --keep-saves`, the five river-valley worlds (seeds 1 to 5, 1024 cells, household fields and village fields in turn) lived fifty years on four cores in 2,237 s, every world's yearly checks passing; 77, 82, 79, 50 and 78 people at the end. **At forty years** (the demo, from the kept saves): all five were council communities under the founding custom, each with a common store at a twentieth and a keeper (the office passing from holder to holder by new laws in three), the levy 86–91 % paid, no relief ever given, no faction, petition, refusal, revolt or coup, and nothing decided in the last two years: one regime, which is what the demo reports. Thorndale (world 1) had decided the most; its oldest decision, opened: Davor proposed a common store at a fifth on 31 July of year 3 "because food would not last until the harvest", and it was turned down two days later, 1 for and 13 against, 19 of 20 adults coming, most against because their household stood to lose though they thought well of the sponsor, talk at the hearth having drawn many of them toward it. **By fifty years one world parted:** Hazelstead (world 4) ran short of food in its fiftieth year; its store gave 12 t of relief in 8,018 asks and fell to 557 kg; 11 died and households left (82 people to 50). In its last two years its gathering decided ten matters, all in the fiftieth: a law against taking, two laws naming keepers of the watch, a curfew; Talia's faction (39 members, against Ferrin as keeper of the store) petitioned for Marek in Ferrin's place, which the gathering granted (35 came); and Marek proposed that the households' elders decide, "because the gathering had decided against them", carried 33 to 0 with 34 of 40 adults coming. Under elders, 19 of its 41 adults may decide: an oligarchy by the classifier's 50 % line, with big-man leadership, Delia having put forward 4 of the 6 laws that passed and all 130 who backed them doing so more for their regard of Delia than for their households. So the regimes row is green, two regimes; the other four stayed council communities. Divergence came late, in one world of five and from a famine, not by year 40; that is what happened. **Rows:** population green (5 of 5 keep 10; the most growth 2.1 times the founders), firm sizes green, structural failures green (1.12 per 1,000 building-years), the Gini of goods amber (0.26–0.41), crime grey (21 attempts, 30 needed), settlement sizes and epidemics grey, regimes green, and **food prices red**: in Hazelstead grain was asked only 1 log point more before the harvest than after, over 40 harvests, against the row's 2 (the other worlds 5 to 8; earlier dashboards 2 to 9; the research's markets 17 to 61). **The red row, probed (time-boxed, not rerun: research 16-03 §4.7):** the gap is a mean over all 40 harvests, and to year 40 Hazelstead's institutions matched the others' (the same custom, store, share and keeper, no relief given), so nothing found ties it to M4c; the row's threshold is a tuning value and every world's gap sits far below the research's range, as it has since slice L. Not tuned toward a pass. **Not built:** a regimes row that weighs the restricted council (50–90 % admitted) apart from the broad one, which the frozen classifier calls one name with a modifier.
- **M5 design (2026-10-09, plan §7):** from four briefs (`docs/briefs/m5-settlements.md`, `m5-diffusion.md`, `m5-trade.md`, `m5-relations.md`), their key citations checked against the reports: 10-01 §5.5 (entity update order must not decide who gets the best land) and its founding buffer (the time to dependable production plus 1–3 months); 05-06 §1.1 (an off-map reservoir must be explicit) and its table of priors (a review every 6 months, 12 destinations, about 2 completed moves per 100 residents a year as an output benchmark, not a quota); 10-02's 0.05–0.20 reconsiderations per household-year ("reconsideration is not migration"); 01-03's one authoritative model; 09-16 §1.2 (a message "is stationary until departure"); 13-01 §1.4 (a council's approval and a ruler's oath "need not be interchangeable"); 08-05 §1.7 (the no-arbitrage band and its 100/130/25 example); 11-02 §2.3 (39 % of the stock after fifty years); 07-02 §1.2 (buying a steel tool does not teach steelmaking). **M5 is split in three** (§7) because its contents are three kinds of work, each with a demo of its own: M5a, several settlements (identities, residence, movement and scale); M5b, trade and diffusion (the demo's prices and roof); M5c, relations and works (treaties, tribute, bridges and enclosures; the demo's failed ratification). **One ADR now,** [ADR-0018](decisions/0018-settlements-residence-movement.md); M5b and M5c are designed when reached, from the same briefs, with one ADR each expected. **The open questions, answered:**
  - **Every daughter founds its own polity** (one to one, ADR-0013 §1). A polity over several settlements waits for M6's conquest, federation and secession.
  - **Leaving the map** keeps its M4a meaning and prior; the off-map account keeps the books, and nobody returns from it.
  - **Reviews:** yearly on a keyed day and at events, and daily when out of food. 05-06's six months and 10-02's 0.05–0.20 a household-year disagree; once a year lies between them (a design prior).
  - **Roads:** M5 builds only crossings. A worn trail already gives full walking speed, so a kept way changes nothing people notice until carts (M8).
  - **World size:** the new-world dialog offers no worlds of thousands until the scale budget is met; benchmarks build them.
  - **Setup groups:** each a band of its own, with its own way of building (so a roof style has somewhere to cross from) and kin only within it. Whether they know one another's sites is a setup choice.
  - **Budget:** a year of 3,000 people in three settlements in at most ten minutes at Max (a design prior); 8,000 measured, not targeted.
  - **Settlement sizes** are reported, not graded, until M9 (10-01 §4 and 10-02 §5 warn against fitting distributions to a few towns).
  - **Climate** stays one series a world: neighbours a few kilometres apart share their weather and lean years, which is also why trade between them may buffer little (08-12 §4).
  - **An exile** goes where they choose, as any leaver does; nothing keeps them out, since no polity yet decides who settles.
  - **The wave:** 5–50 households of one band, walking in from the nearest edge and knowing the settlements near where they are sent.
  - **Marriage between settlements is in** (the diffusion brief expects it to be the commonest carrier at village sizes), the couple placed by land and room, never by sex. **Visits are a behaviour of their own** (company at a known settlement's hearth), besides trade's trips.
  - **Cultures stay derived;** no conformity term is added; esteem replaces goods in admiration only for buildings elsewhere (M5b), so M3b's behaviour at home is unchanged.
  - **Metal:** M5 adds none, so per-polity currencies are not built in M5 (§7).
  - The trade brief's remaining questions (the procurement anchor, scarcity in grain asks, goods in transit, the food-prices row, the demo's twin) and the relations brief's eleven wait for M5b's and M5c's designs.

  **The hearth was everyone's (fixed before the design, `98c9b52`):** `hearth_company` gathered everyone whose target was "the hearth", whichever settlement's hearth they sat at, so with two settlements ties, word, opinions, norms and ideologies passed between people kilometres apart. The diffusion brief found it; a test showed it (a camp of ten families 1,500 m from the village, lived twenty days: hearth ties across the two before the fix, none after); company is now kept with one's own settlement. Worlds of one settlement are unchanged. **Scale, measured (2026-10-09, `bench_village`, seed 2 on 768 cells, on the development container):** 1,000 people lived their first thirty days at 691 ms a day (252 s a year at that rate; slice X's whole year, with its harvest weeks, was 485 s); 3,000 in one village lived their first ten days at 4,105 ms a day, 5.2 times the 1,000's first ten days (785 ms) for three times the people. Three settlements of 1,000 should cost about three times one if searches stay within each (ADR-0018 §7): about 24 minutes a year at slice X's rate, so slice AL needs a further factor of about 2.4. **What would be plot, and is not built:** no settlement size, rate of moves or founding is a target; no timer splits a village or sends a wave; nobody's destination is chosen for them.
- **The save-and-load flake, found (2026-10-08):** a rare failure of the save-and-load continuation tests (about 1 run in 100, first seen in `tests/values.rs` during slice AH) was reproduced by living one world (river valley, seed 3, 512 cells) forty days from 200 fixed identities: two continued differently a day after a load, and only in decision receipts. Traced, one step at a time: the chosen option and every listed total matched, but the chosen option's probability did not; of all the options, breaking new ground scored differently, at another site. A household's candidate new ground is found once a day and kept in a cache the save does not hold. A world lived straight on reused the site found earlier in the day; one loaded found it afresh after a neighbour had marked out a field or plot, so its search saw other land. The cached site is now kept only while what the search reads (the fields, plots and earthworks laid out, and the homes and hearth it keeps clear of) is unchanged. A household therefore also no longer holds to a site that a neighbour's new field has since covered. Afterwards 600 identities (400 of seed 3, 200 of seed 4) continued alike. **Recorded, not changed:** the cache of the building a household would begin (`home_sites`) has the same shape and also reads the household's stores, which change all day; no world showed it, and keying it on its inputs would undo the cache.
- **Revolts, as built (2026-10-08, M4c slice AI step one, ADR-0017 §4):** no new ADR (ADR-0017 §4 decided that a revolt replaces the body through officeholders' own choices and has no roll). **The program:** a faction whose members blame the gathering holds one: of the bodies the content's amendment templates offer that differ from the custom and admit its organizer, the one under which the decisions its members' households were at, under the present custom, would have gone most their way, at each household's stake in each decision (slice AF's test, research 09-02 §3.7; a faction with nothing to gain from any body has no program). Unlike an amendment it may be any body, not only one change away. **Calling** (ADR-0017 §4): at the organizer's monthly review, when neither a petition nor a refusal is called, and when nothing of the faction's own waits or stands, no revolt stands at its settlement and none of its own was called within `petition_days`, its organizer weighs calling on everyone to stand with that body in place of the gathering's. It is worth what a petition is, less `revolt_cost` 0.75 and less what the norms the organizer holds weigh, since the custom itself is what it breaks. **Word** goes from the organizer to its members, then by word of mouth (a fifth claim kind, `revolt`, let go when it ends), and the chronicle records the call. **Sides** (research 04-10 §5.3, 09-11 §2.5: in a crisis people decide daily): each midnight, each adult of the settlement who has heard of it takes a side: with it, with the gathering, or with neither, from their grievance against the gathering, belonging to the faction or else regard for its organizer, the share of those they know who stood with it the day before (for some, more adds less), less what the norms they hold weigh and `w_exclusion` 1.0 for one the new body would leave out (09-11 §1.6); a side needs the points past the gathering's stance margin. **Holding** (09-11 §1.5, §2.2): it holds once the store's keeper and the watch, if any, stand with it and more adults stand with it than with the gathering, for `hold_days` 7 together; then its body decides from now on, a version of the custom *taken* (its `seized_by` names the organizer, no law made it), any amendment in force is superseded, and the chronicle says those who stood with the organizer took the deciding from the gathering, not by its procedure. It comes to nothing when its organizer or faction is gone, or after `revolt_days` 60. Losers keep their wealth, ties and grievances; nothing else changes hands. **The first prior was wrong, as the refusal's was:** with `revolt_cost` 1.0 an organizer all of whose members held the grievance fully, where nobody held the norm, was 0.65 against a threshold of 0.78; at 0.75 (above a refusal's 0.5) it is 0.82. **A finding from the test:** the first test village, with a decision lately gone against the faction, amended its custom by its own procedure (to two-thirds) before the organizer's review, and the old decision then no longer counted, so no revolt was called: the legal route came first, as it should. The test instead gives the village an elders' custom, under which no body one change away would have decided otherwise; there the organizer called a revolt, every one of the 27 who heard of it stood with it, and it held a week later, making a gathering of all the adults, half of whom must come. A second test lets a revolt's time run out: it comes to nothing and the elders' custom stands. Both save and load exactly. **The ten-year smoke passed all 10 worlds** on the first run, with no revolt called: the gathering binds is held about 0.55 on average there, and of the three villages with factions, all petitioned (four petitions, all granted). **Boundary:** saves schema 44 (each custom version's `seized_by`; revolts with their sides in `factions`; 43 still loads), wire 1.43 (each polity's revolts in the Government panel, in kernel words; a version of the custom taken is told so in its history), content API 48 (`revolt_cost`, `revolt_days`, `hold_days`, `w_exclusion`). **Gates:** Gate B and the notables' gate passed. **Not built:** founding after a revolt (the new body deciding which laws and offices stand: step two), a watch of several and coups (step three), and force, damage and violence (step four); an organizer weighing whether others will stand with it before calling (they weigh only their faction's grievance and belonging).
- **M4b design (2026-10-08, plan §7):** from the M4 crime brief, its citations checked against 04-09, 12-04, 09-07 and 09-09. One ADR, [ADR-0015](decisions/0015-incidents-cases-obligations.md): incidents (the kernel's truth), cases (what a polity knows) and beliefs (what people know) are kept and saved apart, and no choice reads an incident; taking is a scored choice behind a moral filter; every sanction is an obligation with a debtor, a beneficiary and a default rule. Four slices, AA to AD (§7). The brief's open questions, answered: **a summary decision by the gathering or an office counts as "no courts"** (plan §7; cases are shaped so that M7 can put a forum between identified and sanctioned). **No corporal punishment or execution** in v0 (settled in the M4a design). **The watch is an office named by a law**, as the storekeeper is: its holders keep watch unpaid, since office pay is not built; a rotating household duty waits for it. **Releasing the store's grain** is relief, built in M4a. **People take only from other households' stores** in v0: not from their own household, standing crops or workshops; whether kin are spared is left to the objection and regard, not a rule. **Sanctions are weighed in days of the household's food**, the scale `Ask` and the levy already use. **A prohibition needs no new good:** a law can close a place or an activity, such as a grove for firewood (AD). The briefs are now kept in the repository (`docs/briefs/`), since the scratch copies were the only ones.
- **Theft and what was seen, as built (2026-10-08, M4b slice AA, ADR-0015 §1–§3, §5, §7):** **Taking** is `Behavior::Take` (appended; `core:activity/take_food`, from 12), weighed only when the household is short, like `Ask`: the food a load would carry against the walk, the person's objection (20 points for a full one), the chance they believe a taker runs of being seen times 10 points (scaled by `exp(−0.3 × risk)` for their risk-taking) and 5 points for full regard for the household's elder. An objection of 0.8 or more rules it out before anything is weighed, with its own exclusion in the receipt (04-09 §5.3). **Objection** is a per-person value, its log-odds drawn as N(2.5, 1.2) for founders (about one in six falls below the filter) and pulled toward the parents' for a child with heritability 0.5, each by a keyed draw of its own so a birth's other draws are unchanged. **The perceived chance of being seen** starts at 0.5 and moves as p̂ ← (1 − α)p̂ + αs: α = 0.3 from one's own attempts (seen or turned back 1, unseen 0) and 0.1 from what one hears (word of a taker 1; a loss found that nobody saw 0). Research 04-09 §5.4 gives no figures for any of this: all are tuning values. **The target** is found as asking finds a giver: the households of the settlement within reach from which a load would carry the most food it wants, the nearest after a walk weighed against regard. **Arrival settles guardianship** (04-09 §1.1, 12-04 §1.4): a member of 10 or more home and awake turns the taker back; so does anyone of 10 or more within 30 m whom the taker notices (0.7 each); a grown sleeper at home wakes with chance 0.25 and the taker flees, seen; otherwise they carry off what they can on a `take` channel, and those about whom they did not notice, and younger children, see them. One who turned back or fled waits 6 to 18 hours (drawn) before weighing it again. **Incidents** (the truth: who, from whom, when, how it ended, what was carried off, who saw) are saved and no choice reads one. **Beliefs** are saved per holder with their source (saw, told, found the loss) and the witness the account began with, so several people repeating one witness are one source. A household finds a taking at the next midnight; a witness tells the household taken from unless they are of the taker's household or regard the taker more than its elder (12-04 §1.2); members share what they know; and at the hearth people tell what they know of takers outside their own household. Beliefs are kept two years. Four tie acts (`took_from_us`, bad provision 3; `took_from_others`, 1; `restored`, good word 1; `refused_restitution`, bad word 2). **A household refuses the asks** of someone it believes took from it, or from a household whose elder a member regards at 0.5 or more. **Responses:** a household that learns who took from it lets it go or demands the food back (a logistic: a point per day of its food taken, less 3 for full regard for the taker); a demand is an obligation due in 30 days, which the taker's household answers once (a logistic: 4 for the share of its settlement's households that believe the taker took, 2 for regard for the household owed, less 0.5 a day of its own food paying costs) and pays from food beyond three days' need each midnight, on a `restitution` channel. It ends met, refused, unpaid when due (the rest an arrear; an award never creates goods) or lapsed. The chronicle tells a taking or an attempt someone saw, and how a demand ended, plainly (06-10 §4). The Takings panel (wire 1.30) shows the latest 60 incidents as what happened beside what people believe and chose, in separate tables joined only by the incident's number. **Deviations from ADR-0015, recorded:** an objection is not yet moved by what the person lives beyond their upbringing (04-09 §1.1's social learning beyond the household waits for M4c's opinion and norms); telling is a rule of ties, not a scored choice, and reporting waits for slice AB, so a household taken from chooses only between letting it go and a demand; and a loss is found at the next midnight rather than at the next use of the store. **Found on the way:** in the first lean-spell probe the taker turned back only for a member at home, so takings made in the evening, with the household at the hearth 20 m off, were "seen" by 33 to 38 people, and the same few people went back to a guarded store dozens of times a day. Research 12-04 §1.4 (an offender may notice a guardian and abandon, or change their timing) and 04-09 §1.1 (capable guardians) gave noticing, the wait and the guardian's age. With a fixed 12-hour wait every retry fell at dawn and in the evening, when households are up, and none of about 170 attempts in 30 days succeeded; the wait is now drawn. **Measured:** in a test's shared shortage (river valley 4 at 512 cells: half the households with nothing, the rest six days' food, every other hungry adult below the filter), one 30-day run saw about 190 attempts by 8 people and at least 4 takings, the rest turned back; takings were seen by neighbours and children; 3 households learnt who took and 2 demanded the food back, unpaid because the takers' households had nothing to spare. The integration test checks the moral filter, that goods are conserved, that every belief naming a taker traces to someone who saw it, that a demand meant to be paid is paid from what can be spared, and an exact save and load. **The ten-year smoke passed all 10 worlds**, twice. On the final build seven had no attempt to take. On the coast, seed 2's village (24 adults) saw 18 attempts by 3 people and 5 takings (82 kg), each seen and known to the household taken from: 5 demands to give it back, 4 met, and 1 ask refused to a taker. Seed 3's 10 attempts by 4 people brought one taking of 3 kg, seen and demanded back; and seed 5's struggling village (11 adults) made 26 attempts by 6 people, none taken. An earlier build's run, with the same rules, had 6, 9 and 17 attempts on three coast worlds and no taking (worlds draw their own identity each run, so runs differ). Gate B passed (20 runs in 1.6 minutes), with no attempt to take in either fixture's third year; takings are reported by run and not graded, since a mean's relative tolerance cannot hold a rare event. The notables' gate passed with the same figures as at M4a. **What it does not show:** the fed villages of the smoke and the gates scarcely take, and the chain from a taking to a demand paid has been seen in one smoke world; whether a lean year in a village of 1,000 makes takings common, or merely makes attempts common, is open.
- **Cases and their decision, as built (2026-10-08, M4b slice AB, ADR-0015 §4–§5):** **The law against taking** is a policy template (`does = "against_taking"`, `core:policy/against_taking`) answering a new issue, `takings` (a household of the settlement found food taken from its store within the year), with sanction bundles people propose: compensation to the household taken from and a fine to the common store, each in days of the taker's household's food, and exile (research 09-07 §1.2 keeps restitution, compensation and a fine apart; §6.2: a bundle, never one severity number; §2.3: burdens in days of household consumption). The core pack has four: restitution alone; three days' compensation; three and a three-day fine; and that with exile. All amounts are tuning values. **The forecast reads no incident:** a household weighs what it would recover (what it lost in the past year to takings whose taker one of its members knows, and the bundle's compensation for each) against what it would owe (what its members took, with compensation, the fine and, for exile, 60 days of its food for the member it would lose, `exile_days`), at the mean chance its grown members believe a taker runs of being seen; the gain is the common store's log-utility forecast of the one less the other. For this a taker now knows what they took (a belief with the new source `did`, which is no account of it) and a household knows what it found missing or brought home (`KnownAmount`, saved). **Households taken from since the last review join those a review reaches** (ADR-0014 §4 extended, recorded here: being taken from reaches a household as running short does), so their elders may propose it. **Bringing a case:** where such a law is in force and the household's chooser knows of it (09-07 §4.1: knowing of a forum is one of access's barriers), a household that learns who took from it chooses by a softmax among letting it go (0 points), a demand (slice AA's points) and a case, worth p̂ × V − C less slice AA's 3 points for full regard for the taker: p̂ = 1 − 0.5^accounts, the chance it believes the gathering would find, from the distinct witnesses its members' beliefs come from (12-04 §1.5: several people repeating one witness are one observation); V a point for each day of food to recover, the bundle's compensation included; C 1 point. Without the law the choice is slice AA's, draw for draw. One whose demand was refused, or unpaid when due, weighs a case after, against letting it go (09-07 §1.1: negotiation can fail and the dispute go on to a forum). The choice now reads what the household found missing and what its members believe, not the incident (slice AA's read the incident's taker and amount, which then always equalled what the household knew). **A case is what the polity knows:** who brought it, whom it accuses, the loss as the household found it, the witnesses behind it, its stage, and the hearing whole; it never reads the incident it is about. **The hearing:** a case goes before the gathering already called, or one called on cases alone for the next evening. Each who came stands for a finding or against it: the one who brought it for, the accused against; everyone else by what they believe (1 point toward a finding if they believe the accused took; otherwise the accounts told there, 2 × (1 − 0.5^leads) − 1 points, so one witness is an even chance and none counts a point against), what their household stands to gain or lose (a point a day of its food, the scale of a demand), and the polity's `w_regard` (1) for the one who brought it less that for the accused, against the 0.25 margin. The body's rule decides: found; not found (turned down or tied); unheard (too few came); or lapsed if a party was gone before it sat. A wrong decision is recorded as made and never repaired. Those who came to a finding then believe it (told, by the one who brought it, from its first witness). **What a finding imposes:** restitution (the loss less what a demand already brought), compensation and a fine to the polity's store, obligations on the accused's household due in 30 days, answered once for the whole finding (slice AA's points for paying, and 2 more for the custom that the gathering binds) and paid in that order from food beyond three days' need, on the `restitution`, `compensation` and `fine` channels; an award never creates goods. Under a bundle that exiles, the one found leaves the valley at once, a migration on their record, not a disappearance (ADR-0015 §5); their household stays and owes what was imposed. **Deviations from ADR-0015, recorded:** the gathering hears every case (no law yet gives the power to an office); labour-days are not built, since no polity work exists to owe; refusing or failing to pay what a finding imposed has no sanction beyond the arrear and the tie acts until a watch exists (slice AC); and exile is carried out by the gathering that found, enforcement by the coalition 09-07 §1.1 says it needs, rather than an obligation the exiled may refuse. **Found on the way:** the hearing first put each household's stake in the common store's forecast, which is flat below a year's subsistence, so in a lean spell every party stood to gain or lose nothing; it is now days of food at a demand's scale, which matters most to those with least. The slice AA integration test had failed about one run in 24, when a lean spell brought no taking in 60 days (guardianship works: one probe saw 58 to 94 attempts and at most one taking in 120 days); both crime tests now set their situation explicitly through a test hook on the rules (the AA test: a taker notices nobody about; the cases test: nobody is a capable guardian either, and a household taken from brings a case), and passed 12 runs of 12. **Measured:** in the cases test's situation (river valley 4, 512 cells, its harshest bundle), two runs saw 5 and 6 takings in about 60 days, all seen and all brought as cases; the gathering, which 13 to 17 of the village's adults came to, found against every accused it heard (10 to 14 for, 2 to 4 against, the opposition the takers' own households), sent each from the valley, and their households refused, or began to pay, what was imposed; a second case against one already exiled lapsed. **The ten-year smoke passed all 10 worlds**, three times. In the first, no attempt to take succeeded anywhere (7, 12 and 28 attempts on three coast worlds), so nothing of this slice arose. In the second, three coast villages each saw one taking, seen and demanded back (one met, two refused), and each passed one law more than its common store and storekeepers account for; the report did not yet name laws against taking, and now does. In the third, three coast villages passed a law against taking after a seen taking, two with restitution alone and one with three days' compensation and a three-day fine. In seed 2's village (26 adults) three takings were seen and demanded back (two met) and one was brought as a case, found, and its restitution paid; in seed 5's struggling village (4 people at the end) one of two takings was brought as a case and found, and the household paid restitution, compensation and the fine; in seed 3's, the household taken from let its one known taking go. No world proposed exile. **Gates:** Gate B passed (20 runs in 1.9 minutes, no attempt to take in either fixture), and the notables' gate passed with its M4a figures (on the coast fixture 3.8 proposals with the tier against 4.0 without), with households taken from now among those a review reaches. **What it does not show:** whether exile is ever chosen, and how often cases come where takings are common (a lean year in a village of 1,000); the smoke's villages take a few times a decade, so each step of the chain has been seen in one or two worlds.
- **The watch and corruption v0, as built (2026-10-08, M4b slice AC, ADR-0015 §6):** **The watch is an office named by a law**, as the storekeeper is: a `keep_watch` policy (`core:policy/keep_watch`) answering `takings`, whose sponsor names the adult they regard most (themselves at full regard). A household weighs it by half of all it found missing in the past year, whoever took it, back to it, against as much of what its own members took (`watch_guard` 0.5, a tuning value; research 12-04 §1.3: households set up a specialization when they expect it to protect their stores). A law now carries its template's kind, set when proposed or loaded and not saved, so the storekeeper's office and the watch are told apart without the catalog; any office lapses when its holder dies or leaves, and the chronicle says which. The office is unpaid (office pay is not built) and has one holder. **Rounds are a chosen activity** (`core:activity/keep_watch`, behaviour `watch`): only for the holder, only in the dark, a set path through the settlement's homes in id order beginning where the last round ended, a 15-minute stand at each of six (12-04 §2.3's 11–15 minute visits, a modern patrol finding, used only as a scale), worth 6 points for the night's first round and less for each since, up to three a night (12-04 §2.1: one account, medium-low confidence). Sleep pressure ends a watcher's night: a test village's watcher walked about 1.7 rounds a night. **Standing at a home the watcher is someone about**, so the guardianship slice AA built does the rest: a taker who notices them turns back, and one who does not is seen. Nothing else makes a watch effective (12-04 §1.4: a patrol affects the places it visits and those who observe it; §2.3: no linear patrol effect). The watch's rounds, hours and the cases it brought are kept on its law, saved, and shown in the Government panel; nothing in the world reads them (12-04 §2.4: capacity is a reported measure). **A watcher who sees a taking chooses once what to do with it** (research 09-09 §1.3: ΔU = B + K − C − M − p̂(F + V)), by a softmax: saying nothing is 0 points; bringing it before the gathering under a law against taking they know of (or, with none, telling those taken from) is 2 points of duty and slice AA's 3 points for full regard for the household taken from, less as much for regard for the taker; and, under such a law, asking the taker's household for three days of its food (at most what it can spare beyond three days' need) to say nothing is worth a point a day of the watcher's own household's food, less 20 points for a full objection to taking what is not theirs (never weighed above the moral filter: asking is taking) and 10 for a certainty of being found out, the same p̂ they hold of takers being seen. The household asked pays (4 points against half a point a day of its food) or refuses, and is then brought before the gathering. A payment moves on a `bribe` channel; a watcher who looked away or was paid keeps their belief to themselves, at home and at the hearth; an unexposed payment lowers their sense of being found out (09-09 §1.5: learning). What a watcher chose is kept as truth (`sightings`, saved) and shown only in the Takings panel's "what happened" layer. **Deviations from ADR-0015 and the brief, recorded:** the watch is one person, not a roster with a backlog (cases wait for the gathering, and the open ones are its backlog); a watcher's silence is never exposed (no audit, rival or victim learns of it in v0, so p̂ only falls), and no office has a removal rule; the collector's diversion (the brief's second corrupt office) is not built; and asking is open only under a law against taking, since without a case there is nothing to spare the taker. **Found on the way:** with no objection to taking, a test's watcher also took, once their household had given its food away to hungry neighbours, and was found and sent from the valley, which ended the watch; the payment test keeps its watcher on watch with a bundle that does not exile. A starving taker's household has nothing to pay with by the midnight a watcher chooses, so a payment is weighed only where it can spare food; and a watcher who has heard of many takings believes being found out likely, so even one without objection usually says nothing rather than ask (about −2 points against 0 in one probe). **Measured:** in the cases test's situation with a watch and a law against taking, the watcher saw 2 to 4 takings and brought each before the gathering (asking scored about −12 for a watcher of ordinary objection); the payment test (a watcher without objection or fear of being found out, and takers' households kept with two days' food) asks, is paid on the `bribe` channel with every good accounted for, or is refused and brings the case; 10 runs of 10 for the six crime tests. **The ten-year smoke passed all 10 worlds**. In one (coast seed 3) the village passed four laws: a common store, a storekeeper, a watch and a law against taking with three days' compensation and a three-day fine. Its watcher walked 4,924 rounds over the decade, 7,386 hours of standing watch (about 1.5 rounds and 2.2 hours a night), saw the one taking that succeeded there, and brought it before the gathering, which found against the taker; nothing it imposed was paid by the decade's end. No other world named a watch. Run times were as before (137 to 192 seconds a world). **Gates:** Gate B passed (20 runs in 1.1 minutes, no attempt to take in either fixture) and the notables' gate passed with its M4a figures. **What it does not show:** whether a watch changes how often takers succeed: in the one smoke village that named one, a single taking came in its years of rounds.
- **Curfews, the crime row and the M4b demo, as built (2026-10-08, M4b slice AD):** **A curfew is a prohibition in the law pipeline** (the M4 crime brief §1.6: "nobody may be off their own plot in set hours"): a `curfew` policy (`core:policy/curfew`) answering `takings`, at the hours its template offers (21:00 to 5:00 and 23:00 to 4:00, tuning values), each a level a sponsor weighs.
